## IBAC — yes it's big, but it's not for you to join

**What IBAC actually is:**

IBAC is the **umbrella organization that represents 15 national/regional business aviation associations at ICAO** (the UN aviation body). It's the highest-level international business aviation governance organization.

**Founded:** 1981
**Headquartered:** Co-located at ICAO headquarters in Montreal
**Status:** Permanent observer at ICAO (gives them voice in setting global aviation standards)
**Structure:** 15 member associations, governing board with reps from each

**Member associations of IBAC include:**
- NBAA (US)
- EBAA (Europe)
- CBAA (Canada)
- AsBAA (Asia)
- AfBAA (Africa)
- ABAG (Brazil — yes, your home country association)
- ABAGE (Argentina)
- BBAA (Brazilian — wait actually ABAG is Brazil)
- And ~10 others by region

**What IBAC actually does:**
- Represents business aviation interests at ICAO globally
- Manages **IS-BAO** (International Standard for Business Aircraft Operations) — operator safety standard
- Manages **IS-BAH** (International Standard for Business Aviation Handling) — ground handling standard
- Issues **IBAC Aircrew Identification Cards** — used worldwide for security/airport access
- Coordinates ICAO advocacy for business aviation
- Carbon Credit Exchange for carbon offsetting

**Membership categories:**
- National/regional associations (the 15 main members)
- **Industry Partners** (added 2016)
- **Operator Affiliates** (added 2016)

## Can OSA join IBAC?

**Theoretically yes via Industry Partner category, but it doesn't make sense.**

IBAC is the policy/advocacy umbrella. They don't run conferences for industry networking. They don't have member directories useful for prospecting. They don't have transaction-relevant resources.

**What IBAC is good for:**
- Policy advocacy if you operate internationally
- IS-BAO certification for your own operations (if OSA Connect operates aircraft eventually)
- Carbon offsetting infrastructure
- Aircrew ID Cards if you have flight crew

**What IBAC is NOT good for:**
- Networking with IADA dealers
- Building US-Brazil corridor relationships
- Getting customer leads
- Industry visibility at the operator level

**Honest take:** IBAC is upstream regulatory advocacy. Important for the industry overall, but not where OSA needs to be at this stage. Membership through ABAG (your Brazilian national association) gives OSA implicit IBAC representation without needing direct membership.

You'd consider IBAC Industry Partner status much later — Year 7+ — when OSA operates internationally and wants policy voice. Not now.

## EBAA — yes, but with caveats given recent news

**What EBAA is:**

European equivalent of NBAA. The leading business aviation organization in Europe.

**Founded:** 1977
**Headquartered:** Brussels, Belgium
**Members:** 700+ companies covering entire business aviation value chain
**Combined fleet:** 1,000+ aircraft

**Big news:** **EBAA just cancelled EBACE 2026** (would have been June 2-4, 2026 in Geneva). This is genuinely significant. EBACE was Europe's largest business aviation trade show. The cancellation was announced around December 2025 because "EBACE26 format did not generate the momentum needed to deliver a viable edition."

This is meaningful context. EBACE was always positioned as Europe's NBAA-BACE equivalent. Its cancellation suggests:
- European business aviation is under political/regulatory pressure (sustainability protests, etc.)
- The event format isn't generating commercial momentum
- EBAA is restructuring its engagement model

**EBAA still exists and operates** — just not running EBACE 2026. They're focusing resources on EU advocacy, regulatory work, and member services.

## Is EBAA worthy for OSA?

**Honest answer: not at this stage.**

Reasoning:
1. **OSA's market is US-Brazil corridor, not Europe**
2. **Without EBACE 2026, the main networking event is gone**
3. **EU regulatory advocacy isn't relevant to OSA's current operations**
4. **European business aviation transactions are different market than US**
5. **Your beachhead is post-raise founders + US-Brazil, not European HNW**

EBAA membership would make sense IF:
- OSA expands operations into European market
- OSA Aircraft starts doing European-registered aircraft transactions
- You want EU regulatory voice
- Loud Hawk wants European reader base

None of those are current priorities.

**EBACE26 cancellation actually reinforces:** Don't bet on European market right now. The signal from EBAA itself is that European business aviation is in a difficult position.

## What this all means strategically

Here's the cleaner picture of which associations matter for OSA at different stages:

### Now (Year 1-2): Foundation
**Definitely join:**
- **NBAA** ($95-300/year) — US table stakes
- **ABAG** (Brazilian Business Aviation Association) — home market, gives implicit IBAC representation

**Investigate joining:**
- **EJOA** (Embraer specialty, $1,000-2,000/year estimated) — talk to Jamie Rogers
- **AERA** (Brazilian aircraft resellers) — when OSA Aircraft transactions start

**Cost: $1,500-3,500/year for foundation positioning.**

### Year 2-4: Specialization
**Add when relevant:**
- **NAFA** when OSA Pay positioning matures (~$1,000-2,500/year)
- **CJP** if OSA expands to Citation specialty
- **POPA** if OSA expands to Pilatus specialty
- **ISTAT regular member** when pursuing appraisal credentials

### Year 4-7: Industry presence
**Add when scale justifies:**
- **IADA Verified Products & Services Member** if OSA's data platform achieves IADA dealer adoption
- **NBAA committee participation**
- **ABAG board involvement**

### Year 7+: Leadership tier
**Add when established:**
- **IBAC Industry Partner** if OSA operates internationally
- **ICAO observer participation** through ABAG
- **IADA Accredited Dealer** if OSA Aircraft transaction volume justifies
- **EBAA membership** if European expansion happens

## The associations that DON'T matter for OSA

To be efficient about where you spend dues:

**Skip these unless specific reason:**
- IBAC direct membership (covered through ABAG)
- EBAA (European market, EBACE26 cancelled, not your beachhead)
- AsBAA (Asian market, not your focus)
- AfBAA (African market, not your focus)
- CBAA (Canadian market, very small)
- All the type-specific associations except EJOA + maybe CJP/POPA

## The bigger picture

The industry has roughly 50+ associations across regional, national, type-specific, and functional dimensions. **Most of them don't matter for OSA's specific positioning.**

Your strategic associations align with:
1. **Specialty:** Embraer (EJOA)
2. **Geography:** US (NBAA) + Brazil (ABAG)
3. **Function:** Aircraft transactions (eventually IADA, AERA, ISTAT)
4. **Operations:** Finance (NAFA when relevant)

Five associations max for OSA's positioning. Maybe seven if NAFA, ISTAT, and AERA all become relevant. That's the right scope.

**IBAC is too high-level for you. EBAA is too European-focused. Both are real, important organizations — just not for OSA at this stage.**

The associations that ACTUALLY matter for your positioning are the ones I've consistently recommended:
- NBAA (now)
- EJOA (now-soon)
- ABAG (now)
- NAFA (Year 2-3)
- ISTAT (Year 3+)
- IADA (Year 5+)

That's the focused stack. Skip the rest unless specific operational reason emerges.

## CJI = Corporate Jet Investor

**It's a media company, not an association.** This is the important distinction.

CJI is owned by **Specialist Insight** (UK-based publisher) and operates as a B2B media + events company specifically focused on the business aviation transaction industry. Think of it as the "deal Bloomberg" of business aviation.

## What CJI actually does

**Three main products:**

**1. Conferences (the main thing)** — global event circuit:

- **CJI London** — flagship event, ~600 delegates, 3 days, Regent's Park area
- **CJI Miami** — November 16-18, 2026, Fontainebleau Miami Beach, ~550 attendees
- **CJI Latin America (São Paulo)** — April 27-28, 2026, Renaissance São Paulo
- **CJI Dubai** — Middle East/Africa focus, ~310 attendees, Ritz-Carlton JBR
- **CJI Asia (Singapore)** — September, Raffles City
- **CJI India (Delhi)** — newly launched
- **CJI Cape Town** — Africa focus, April 2027
- **CJI Saudi Arabia** — Saudi/MENA focus
- **SAF Investor London** — Sustainable Aviation Fuel focus
- **Revolution.Aero** — innovation/eVTOL focus
- **Dealmakers Club** — invitation-only networking receptions during major events

**2. Media products:**

- **CorporateJetInvestor.com** — daily news website
- **CJIQ** — quarterly magazine, print + digital
- **One Minute Week** — weekly news analysis email
- **The Official Guide to Aircraft Registration** — reference publication
- **The Official Guide to Sustainable Business Aviation** — sustainability reference

**3. Intelligence/data products:**

- **SemaphoreIntel.com** — aviation intelligence platform
- **Online Aircraft Transaction Masterclass** — training course
- Focus group services for aviation companies

## Who attends CJI conferences

**Key attendee profile** (this is what matters for OSA):

CJI specifically targets **dealmakers** — the people who actually buy, sell, finance, and trade aircraft. Not pilots, not flight departments, not enthusiasts. Specifically:

- Aircraft brokers/dealers (heavy IADA presence)
- OEMs (Bombardier, Textron, Embraer, Gulfstream, Dassault)
- Aircraft financiers (Global Jet Capital, JSSI, banks)
- Aviation lawyers (top legal firms)
- MROs and engine programs (JSSI dominant)
- Appraisers (ISTAT/ASA certified)
- Aircraft managers (NetJets, Flexjet, etc.)
- FBOs (Signature, Jet Aviation, etc.)
- Tax/escrow specialists (TVPX, Aero-Space Reports)
- Insurance specialists

**It's the deal-doers room.** Not industry generally. Specifically transaction-focused.

## Why CJI is genuinely strategic for OSA

This is the most relevant industry event ecosystem for what OSA Aircraft is trying to build. Here's why:

**1. Transaction focus matches OSA's positioning**
CJI's whole identity is aircraft transactions — the exact market OSA is building for. Unlike NBAA-BACE which covers everything from charter to charter operators to flight departments to manufacturers, CJI is laser-focused on dealmakers.

**2. CJI Latin America (São Paulo) is your target event**
April 27-28, 2026 at Renaissance São Paulo. This is genuinely the most relevant conference for OSA. Brazilian aviation transaction community concentrated. ~300 attendees from CJI Latin America 2024.

**This is where you'd meet:**
- Marcelo Simoni (Holstein Aviation, IADA Certified Broker, Brazilian)
- Rogério Andrade (Avantto CEO)
- José Brandão (Synerjet CEO)
- Philipe Figueiredo (Timbro Trading)
- Pedro Ferreira (Timbro Trading)
- Bonsenso Advogados (aviation lawyers)
- Bernardi & Schnapp Advogados (aviation lawyers)
- Regional Embraer execs
- Latin American HNW family office reps
- US brokers with Latin America presence

**3. CJI is IADA-adjacent**
Heavy IADA dealer attendance. IADA executives speak. CJI London is now CPD/CLE accredited "with CPD Group and IADA" specifically. The CJI/IADA relationship is institutional.

**4. Open registration**
Unlike IADA member events, anyone can register. No sponsorship required.

**5. Quality of attendees**
600 delegates at CJI London. 550 at CJI Miami. These are senior dealmakers, not random attendees. The selection bias is built in by the price point.

## CJI Latin America 2026 specifically — your priority event

April 27-28, 2026, Renaissance São Paulo. Pre-conference welcome reception April 26.

Looking at past CJI Latin America agendas:

**Topics covered:**
- "Opportunities in South American business aviation" (panel with Synerjet, Avantto, Aeromot)
- Trading companies and importation (Timbro, Razac Trading)

**Estimated cost:**
~$2,000-3,500 for registration. Plus travel. Worth it given combined family visit potential to São José dos Campos.

## How CJI fits in OSA's strategy

Different events have different roles:

**Conferences for INDUSTRY-WIDE presence:**
- NBAA-BACE October — biggest, broadest
- EBACE Geneva — European (now cancelled for 2026, watch space)

**Conferences for TRANSACTION/DEAL FOCUS:**
- CJI London — flagship deal-focused event

**Conferences for SPECIALTY/AUDIENCE:**
- EJOA Annual — Embraer specialty
- POPA — Pilatus specialty

CJI fills a specific role: **the deal-doer's conference circuit.** Different from owner association meetings, different from broad industry events, different from specialty type meetings.

## The strategic answer for OSA

**For OSA's positioning as US-Brazil aircraft transaction specialist, CJI Latin America is genuinely the highest-leverage single event you could attend.**

Reasons:
1. Transaction focus matches OSA Aircraft positioning
2. Brazilian aviation industry concentrated

4. 300 attendees is intimate enough for actual relationship building
5. Marcelo Simoni and others would attend
6. IADA-adjacent without IADA gates
7. Worth $2,000-3,500 investment

**Probably more valuable than NBAA-BACE for your specific positioning.**

NBAA-BACE is broader and bigger but you'd be one of 25,000 attendees. CJI Latin America is 300 attendees, specifically Brazilian aviation focus. The signal-to-noise ratio for OSA is dramatically higher.

## Recommended event priority for OSA Year 1

If you can only do 2-3 events in 2026:

**Priority 1: CJI Latin America 2026** (April 27-28, São Paulo)
- Highest leverage for OSA positioning
- Combined family visit
- Brazilian aviation industry concentrated
- ~$2,000-3,500

**Priority 2: NBAA-BACE 2026** (October 21-23, Las Vegas)
- US table stakes
- IADA member booth access
- Broad industry presence
- ~$1,500-2,500

**Priority 3 (if budget allows): CJI Miami 2026** (November 16-18)
- Latin America corridor reinforcement
- Strong Brazilian/Latin American attendance
- Adjacent to NBAA-BACE timing
- ~$2,000-3,000

These three give you:
- US industry presence (NBAA)
- Latin America transaction industry (CJI LatAm)
- Cross-corridor reinforcement (CJI Miami)

**Total investment: $5,500-9,000 for three events** that genuinely matter for OSA's positioning.

**One Minute Week** — their free weekly newsletter. Quick aviation industry news digest. Worth subscribing to track market dynamics, new deals, who's hiring, etc. Free.

**CJIQ magazine** — quarterly print magazine. Probably worth subscribing for industry intelligence and content inspiration for Loud Hawk.

**CorporateJetInvestor.com** — daily news. Good source for Loud Hawk content references.

## Bottom line

**CJI is the dealmakers' media + events company.** Not an association you join — a publisher and conference operator you attend and engage with.

For OSA specifically:

## Things we haven't covered yet

### 1. ARGUS / Wyvern — charter safety auditing

This is genuinely a gap. We mentioned ARGUS briefly but didn't go deep. For OSA Connect specifically:

**ARGUS has three tiers** for charter operators:
- **ARGUS Gold** — basic safety audit
- **ARGUS Platinum** — enhanced standards
- **ARGUS Platinum Elite** — top tier (NetJets, Flexjet level)

**ARGUS Certified Charter Broker** — separate credential for charter brokers (different from aircraft sales brokers). This is critical for OSA Connect to serve Fortune 500 flight departments and family offices. Many corporate clients require ARGUS Certified Broker as minimum standard.

**Wyvern** is the alternative — Wyvern Wingman / Wyvern PASS Broker. Some clients prefer Wyvern audits over ARGUS.

For OSA Connect's actual operations, ARGUS Certified Broker is **probably more important than IADA Verified P&S Member.** It's directly customer-facing — Fortune 500 procurement won't book charter through non-ARGUS-certified brokers.

We should map ARGUS path specifically.

### 2. WAI / WCA / IAWA — Women in aviation associations

Not directly relevant to you, but worth knowing because **these networks are increasingly influential in business aviation.** Women in Corporate Aviation (WCA), Women in Aviation International (WAI), International Aviation Women's Association (IAWA). Major IADA dealers participate. Loud Hawk content opportunity to profile women in the industry.

### 3. NBAA-CAM credential — we've mentioned but not deep-dived

NBAA-CAM (Certified Aviation Manager) is genuinely the most accessible major credential because:
- Open application
- 100-point system you can map exactly
- Your ATP/CFI/CFII/MEI maxes out the licensing/certification points category
- Charter brokerage operations count toward experience
- Recertification every 36 months

Worth mapping the exact NBAA-CAM application path for you specifically.

### 4. Speaking circuit / industry voice

This is genuinely missing. Becoming a recognized industry voice through:
- **Speaking at conferences** (CJI panels, NBAA sessions, ISTAT keynotes)
- **Podcasts** — there are several aviation industry podcasts (AssetInsight, BizAv Talks, Jet Set, etc.)
- **Bylined articles** — Aviation International News (AIN), Business Jet Traveler, Corporate Jet Investor magazines accept contributed articles
- **NBAA Top 40 Under 40** nomination — major industry recognition (you're 32, perfect timing window)

For OSA's positioning, becoming the recognized "AI-native aviation transaction expert" voice would be massively valuable. This is faster ROI than credentials.

### 5. Aviation lawyers — your future critical relationships

We mapped credentials but not legal counsel relationships. For OSA Aircraft to operate, you need:
- US aviation law firm (Greenberg Traurig, Jackson & Wade, Aerlex Law Group, Gilchrist Aviation Law, Daniels Long & Pinsel)
- Brazilian aviation law firm (Bonsenso Advogados, Bernardi & Schnapp, Basch & Rameh)
- International aviation law firm for cross-border (Vedder Price)
- Title/escrow specialists (TVPX, Aero-Space Reports, Insured Aircraft Title Service)

These firms are themselves IADA Verified P&S Members in many cases. Building relationships with aviation legal community creates natural pathway to IADA dealer relationships.

### 6. Aviation insurance specialists

OSA's four-policy stack (GL, E&O, Cyber, Tech E&O/AI Liability) — but the aviation insurance brokers who specialize in business aviation are themselves a network worth knowing. **Aviation specialty insurance brokers** like:

These brokers transact every aircraft purchase and know everyone in the industry. They're natural relationship hubs.
